## 3. Per-test diagnosis and exact fix

### A. Vulnerable packages (TR-MCP-QUALITY-001; operator: use the recommended packages)

- Cause: `Directory.Packages.props:91` pins `System.Security.Cryptography.Xml` 10.0.10, which brings `System.Security.Cryptography.Pkcs` 10.0.10. All five advisories name 10.0.13 as the first patched 10.x version (`gh api /advisories/<id>`, 14:35:12); nuget.org lists 10.0.13 as newest for both (14:36:23); both 10.0.13 nuspecs target net8.0, net9.0, net10.0 and netstandard2.0 (14:41:31).
- Fix: Xml 10.0.10 to 10.0.13 at `Directory.Packages.props:91`; add `<PackageVersion Include="System.Security.Cryptography.Pkcs" Version="10.0.13" />` (transitive pinning is on, `Directory.Packages.props:4`, so every project takes it). No `WarningsNotAsErrors`, `NoWarn`, `NuGetAudit` or audit-source change anywhere, and no override in any run from here on.
- Proof: a clean Nuke `Restore` and `Compile` on both hosts in fresh worktrees with zero NU1901 to NU1904 entries.

### B. Stale backslash expectations from the forward-slash key change (FR, TR and TEST-MCP-PATHNORM-001; oracle `ExpectedWorkspaceKey.Of`)

Production already implements FR-MCP-PATHNORM-001; these tests still assert the superseded spelling or depend on the host's `Path.GetFullPath`. Test-only fixes, each with a negative control (the old value rejected inside a passing harness).

- B1 `UseCaseGetWorkspaceResolutionContractTests.ProductionGate_EquivalentRawPath_UsesCanonicalWorkspace` (both hosts; TEST-MCP-USECASE-002): expects `F:\GitHub\TruckMate`, gets `F:/GitHub/TruckMate` because `WorkspaceKeyConversion` canonicalizes on save (`src/QBrainAi.Storage/WorkspaceKeyConversion.cs:24-26`). Fix: split the constant into `RawWorkspacePath = @"F:\GitHub\TruckMate"` (seed and query input) and `CanonicalWorkspaceId = "F:/GitHub/TruckMate"` (expected value and mock result). Negative control `MockGate_BackslashWorkspaceId_IsRejected`.
- B2 `RemoveTodoRecordsMigrationTests` x2 (both hosts; TEST-MCP-TRIAGE-004) and `Decompose4nfBackfillMigrationTests.Migrate_Decompose4nf_BackfillsJsonListColumnsIntoChildTables` (Category=Integration; Windows MigrationIntegrationTests): raw-SQL seeds write `Q:\__mcp_unit_test__\QBrainAi` and `C:\ProgramData\QBrainAi`; the migrations copy keys verbatim (`20260628122208_RemoveTodoRecords.cs`, `20260628194717_RepairTriageCreatedTodoWorkspace.cs`), but the LINQ predicates are converted to the canonical `Q:/...` form, so `Single` finds nothing. Fix: seed the canonical form (`Q:/__mcp_unit_test__/QBrainAi`, `C:/ProgramData/QBrainAi`), which is what a store holds under FR-MCP-PATHNORM-001. Rewriting pre-existing backslash rows is federation P1.5 (`--workspace-path-rekey`, its named test `WorkspaceRekeyRewritesStoredBackslashKeysThreeProviders`), not this effort.
- B3 `WorkspacePathNormalizerRealTests.ProcessNormalizer_ForeignAbsolutePath_IsNotReRooted` (Linux; TEST-MCP-FED-PATH-001): expects `C:\Users\kingd` unchanged, gets `C:/Users/kingd`. Fix: `Assert.Equal(ExpectedWorkspaceKey.Of(foreign), result)`; the not-re-rooted assertion stays.
- B4 `WorkspaceServiceAccessorTests` x2 (Linux; TEST-HANDOFF-006): expectations wrap `Path.GetFullPath(@"F:\first")`, which re-roots a foreign path under the Linux working directory; production deliberately does not (`HandoffWorkspacePaths.cs:37`). Fix: `ExpectedWorkspaceKey.Of(@"F:\first")` and the same for each literal.
- B5 `GitHubCliServiceTests` x2 (Linux; TEST-MCP-083): the repo roots `C:\workspace` and `C:\repo\workspace` are foreign on Linux, so the expected `Path.GetFullPath("C:\\workspace")` and the service's `Path.GetFullPath("C:/workspace")` (`GitHubCliService.cs:782-788`) differ only in the re-rooted separator. Fix: host-native temporary workspace directories, as the passing test at `GitHubCliServiceTests.cs:461` already does; expected `WorkingDirectory == Path.GetFullPath(workspacePath)`.
- B6 `WorkspaceResolutionMiddlewareTests` x3 (Linux; TR-MCP-MT-002): `ApiKey_ResolvesWorkspace_WhenNoHeader`, `EmptyHeader_SkipsToApiKeyFallback`, `DefaultToken_SetsIsDefaultKey`. The token service stores the canonical key `C:/projects/alpha`; the test's `IWorkspaceService` substitute matches with `Path.GetFullPath` on both sides, which on Linux never equates `C:/...` with `C:\...`. The real `WorkspaceService.GetAsync` normalizes both through the path normalizer (`WorkspaceService.cs:101-111, 644-645`). Fix: the substitute's matcher compares `ExpectedWorkspaceKey.Of` of both values, ordinal ignore case.

### C. Provider tests are integration tests (operator: tag them Integration)

- Verified: `SessionLogAgentSessionHeaderMigrationTests.SqlServerUpSql_LegacySessionLogs_AddsHeaderColumnsAndTagsIdempotently` opens LocalDB or `MCP_TEST_SQLSERVER_CONNECTION` (`:215-235`); `PostgreSqlUpSql_LegacySessionLogs_AddsHeaderColumnsAndTagsIdempotently` boots `EphemeralPostgresFixture` (`:305-319`); `EphemeralPostgresFixtureTests.EphemeralPostgresFixture_InitdbFailure_CapturesSanitizedInvocationStdoutAndStderr` runs `initdb.exe` or adopts an external server (`:23-53`). All three passed on Windows in the diagnostic `Test` run.
- Fix: method-level `[Trait("Category", "Integration")]` on the two UpSql tests (their class also holds SQLite unit tests) and class-level on `EphemeralPostgresFixtureTests`. They leave the unit `Test` filter (`build/Build.Test.cs:50,63`) and run in `MigrationIntegrationTests` (`build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:63-129`).

### D. The two Windows skips (TEST-MCP-PATHNORM-001)

- `WorkspaceNativePathMockDataTests.NonWindowsHost_IsUnchanged` and `WorkspaceNativePathRealTests.NonWindowsHost_IsUnchanged` skip on Windows (`Assert.SkipWhen(OperatingSystem.IsWindows(), ...)`, `WorkspaceNativePathContractTests.cs:42`) because `WorkspaceIdentityPath.ToNativePath` reads `OperatingSystem.IsWindows()` directly (`src/QBrainAi.Client/WorkspaceIdentityPath.cs:1043-1049`).
- Fix: add `ToNativePath(string path, WorkspacePathPlatform platform, IWorkspaceHostEnvironment host)` (decides on `host.Platform`); the existing two-argument method delegates with `SystemWorkspaceHostEnvironment.Instance`, so its behavior is unchanged. Move the non-Windows-host case into a new contract `WorkspaceNativePathHostContractTests` run against a simulated non-Windows host (`SimulatedWorkspaceHosts`) on every runner, with MockData and Real subclasses, so it executes on Windows too. Public API addition in the published `QBrainAI.Client` package: additive, not breaking.
- The 16 Linux skips (the Windows-only cases in `WorkspaceNativePathContractTests` and `WorkspaceRemotePathContractTests`) stay as they are, per the operator. Superseded by section 9: the native-path Windows-host cases run on every host against the simulated host, and the remote-path Real binding is a Windows integration test without a skip.

### E. Windows evidence route environment (not code)

- `RequirementsTwelfthAdversarialReviewTests.OpenFileForRead_DirectoryAliasSwap_NeverReadsExternalObject` and `OpenFileForWrite_DirectoryAliasSwap_NeverWritesExternalObject` fail with Win32Exception "start process 'pwsh.exe' ... cannot find the file"; `BugTriage139FourteenthHarnessTests.ProcessRunner_ExitedParentWithPipeHoldingDescendant_KillsTreeWithinBound` gets Win32Exception instead of TimeoutException and starts `pwsh.exe` on Windows (`:100-102`). Observed: `pwsh.exe` is not on the lab-wsman PATH; `$PSHOME` is `C:\Program Files\PowerShell\7-LabWsman` and holds `pwsh.exe`. In Build.Tests, `YamlObjectMutationTests` (start 'pwsh') and both `RequirementsWikiPublishScriptTests` (start 'pwsh.exe') have the same cause.
- Fix in the remote runner only: prepend `$PSHOME` to `PATH`, and keep `MCP_NUKE_POWERSHELL_BOOTSTRAPPED=1` because `build.ps1:10-24` re-launches through `wsmprovhost.exe` in a WSMan session (filed as `triage-report-aa178657cf1140e59e4065d10b3c67d5`). Inference to confirm on the first Windows run: these five [27.42, eighteenth read B8: six; the line above names three Support.Mcp cases, `YamlObjectMutationTests` and both `RequirementsWikiPublishScriptTests`] pass once `pwsh.exe` resolves.

### F. Open diagnosis: `HostileReviewG4OverlayTests.HostileReviewWorker_InvalidOptions_FailStartup` (Windows only; TR-MCP-HOSTILEREVIEW-003)

- Observed: `host.StartAsync` throws, but `ex.ToString()` lacks "HostileReviewWorker"; the TRX shows only "System.AggregateException: An error occurred while"··· (`:474-475`). The cause is not known. Closed in section 10 by `b2319488` (PLAN-11): the full exception text read "An error occurred while writing to logger(s). (Cannot open log for source '.NET Runtime'. You may not have write access.)". The default host builder adds the Event Log provider on Windows, and an account without Event Log write access failed startup in the logger before options validation could report. The test clears the default logging providers, and its assertion is unchanged.
- Step: make the assertion report the full text (`Assert.True(ex.ToString().Contains("HostileReviewWorker", StringComparison.Ordinal), ex.ToString())`; same condition, not weaker), rerun the Windows gate, then fix by cause. A production cause comes back to you as a plan amendment before any production edit.
- Since round 6 (section 19): the test is `Category=Integration` on both hosts, because its default host builder reads the process environment (TR-MCP-USECASE-018 AC-006, TR-MCP-USECASE-019 AC-008 as strengthened); its assertion is unchanged.

### G. `MigrationIntegrationTests` target-level failures on Windows (operator decision, asked separately)

- Decompose4nf: fixed by B2.
- Build.Tests, 8 Linux-only cases of `ServiceUpdateTests` (`LinuxPreflight_RejectsSpecialApphostAndTextInputs` x4, `LinuxPreflight_RejectsSpecialStageFiles`, `LinuxPreflight_RejectsSymlinkRootAndNonexecutableStage(symlink: False)`, `LinuxArchive_UsesPrivateMetadataPreservingCommands`, `LinuxReplacement_DoesNotFollowSymlinks`): `mkfifo` missing, "Unix file modes are not supported on this platform", access denied on the stale-file replacement. They cannot pass on Windows, and the target cannot run on Linux because `InstallTestDependencies` installs the Windows LocalDB MSI (`build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:236-272`). So these 8 pass on no host today. Superseded: TR-MCP-NUKE-018 (taken over in `11681680`) runs the target on Linux, and amendment G runs these cases only there.
- Build.Tests, 3 PATH cases: fixed by E.

## 11. Amendment H: the Windows integration lane runs interactively (H1 approved 18:47 [27.42, eighteenth read C7: asked 18:47:22 (question stamped 18:47:02), answered 18:54:10 (transcript lines 10294 and 10295)])

- Operator, 18:45: "Lane runs interactively by design". Requirement first: TR-MCP-NUKE-019 AC-5, recorded 18:46 and unsatisfied: "On a Windows host, MigrationIntegrationTests runs its test processes in an interactive desktop session of the logged-on user, so a Windows case that needs an interactive session (Environment.UserInteractive is true) executes and passes; a run that cannot reach an interactive session fails before any test runs and names the missing session, and never reports such a case as passed, skipped, or not executed."
- Why: `DesktopProcessSpawnerWindowsInteractiveTests` asserts an interactive session; the WSMan evidence route runs as lab-wsman in a non-interactive session, so the Windows lane stays at 1 failed.
- Approach, your decision (each meets AC-5):
  - H1 (recommended): interactive precondition. On Windows the target checks its own process before any test runs and fails, naming the missing session, when it is not interactive. The Windows lane is then run from a console in kingd's desktop session (a PowerShell.Mcp console or a terminal), by you or an agent working there; I read its TRX back over WSMan as the receipt. Blast radius: one pure decision function and its binding in `build/Build.TestDependencies.cs`; no change to the machine. Consequence: every Windows lane run needs a session on the desktop; the WSMan route still runs the Windows `Test` target and proves the precondition fails as specified.
  - H2: relaunch into the desktop session. Invoked without an interactive session, the target registers a one-shot scheduled task that runs the same `dotnet test` commands in the logged-on user's interactive session, waits for it, collects the TRX files, and removes the task. Blast radius: task registration code in `build/`, rights for lab-wsman to register a task that runs as kingd, kingd logged on during every run, and a transient scheduled task on each run. Consequence: WSMan stays the whole evidence route. Unknown until tried: whether lab-wsman can register an interactive task for kingd without kingd's password.
- Tests first (Build.Tests, raw `dotnet test`): the decision as a pure function (host platform and interactive flag in; run, or fail with the named message, out); contract scenarios for Linux (unchanged), Windows interactive (run) and Windows non-interactive (fail before tests); scripted implementation with negative controls (runs when non-interactive, skips instead of failing, message omits the session, fails on Linux); mock gate; then production and the binding. Under H2, the task command line is composed by a pure function with the same pattern, and the registration itself is exercised only by the lane run.
- Gates: Linux lane unchanged at 0 failed; Windows lane 0 failed and 0 not executed from an interactive session; a non-interactive Windows invocation fails before any test with the named message (receipt from the WSMan route); full Build.Tests 0 failed.
- Approved 18:47 [27.42, eighteenth read C7: asked 18:47:22 (question stamped 18:47:02), answered 18:54:10 (transcript lines 10294 and 10295)] ("Approve H1 (Recommended)") and built as `69d8f3f5`: mock gate `unit-green-h-mockgate-20261019T1849` [27.42, eighteenth read D15: the id's stamp is not its start; it ran 18:55:03 to 18:55:08, after the 18:54:10 approval (scratch run record `runs/unit-green-h-mockgate-20261019T1849.meta.json`, not a committed receipt)] 9/9; production and binding `unit-green-h-real-20261019T1856` 14/14; Linux lane `unit-green-h-int-20261019T1856` 153 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped; Windows over WSMan `unit-green-win-h-wsman-20261019T1856` refused before any test with the named message and wrote no TRX.

## 15. Hostile validation round 2 and the structural fix (2026-10-20)

- Round 2, gate `unitgreen-r2`, run by Codex gpt-6-astra at max:
  - Verdict: DISAGREE. Accuracy 96, completeness 94; 7 PASS, 6 FAIL, 0 UNKNOWN. Every FAIL is classified as an implementer edit.

  - The reviewer's own turn holds the entire result: `req-20261020T023148Z-unitgreen-r2` in session `Codex-20261020T023103Z-unitgreen-r2`.
  - Closure of round 1: F1, F4, F5 and F6 PASS; F2, F3 and F7 FAIL.
  - Findings: R2-F1, environment-dependent unit tests survived because the sweep followed sandbox failures instead of dependencies (two link-creating tests and two repository-reading Repl.Core tests named). R2-F2, the line-based scanner missed a member call split after the dot and qualified or listed OS attributes. R2-F3, the generated Windows runner was not analyzed before it ran, and the round 2 self-review said it was.
- Goal stop condition (a second DISAGREE in one design area) reached; the operator chose on 2026-10-20: "Structural fix, round 3 (Recommended)". Later directions the same morning: "Use the latest Roslyn tools!", "Looks like an opportunity to DRY" (the repository-root walk-ups), "I need you to get your goal completed without further scope creep.", "Pause the C4 agent until your goal is complete", and "Please use ssh with the kingd key for execution on Legion2 so you have full access to the contents of the kingd home folder."
- R2-F2, the scanner on Roslyn syntax trees (`45a3197c`):
  - `UnitSuitePlatformScanner` reads the syntax tree (Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp) instead of regex lines, and the hand-written lexer is removed. A member access split across lines, a qualified, global-qualified or aliased name (aliases resolved file-wide here; in their lexical scope since round 4, section 17; alias chains and static imports of an alias are a declared limit, section 18), a `using static` import, a method group, an OS attribute anywhere in a list or on the assembly, a data-row or `SkipExceptions` skip, `Skip.If`, and code in every conditional-compilation branch (each symbol combination parsed; more than 8 symbols fails the scan) are found; a name inside `nameof` is not a use; a method-level Integration trait is honored.
  - The central Roslyn pins moved from 5.3.0 to 5.9.0, the newest stable on nuget.org, for all five packages (production library use in Storage and Support.Mcp included), per "Use the latest Roslyn tools!" and the existing central package management standard.
  - BDPv4: 24 scripted cases with round 1, 2 and 3 negative controls and the symbol limit came first. Mock gates `ug-r3-mockgate-1` (Roslyn 5.3.0) and `ug-r3-mockgate-2` (5.9.0): Nuke Test 4602 total, 4602 passed, 0 failed, 0 not executed.
- R2-F1, classification by dependency and one repository-root locator (`27c43bcc`):
  - `tests/Shared/TestRepositoryRoot.cs` is the one repository-root locator (`MCP_REPOSITORY_ROOT`, then the `McpRepositoryRoot` build metadata, then the marker walk-up), linked into Support.Mcp.Tests, Repl.Core.Tests and QBAgent.Tests. It resolves only when the running test case or class carries `Category=Integration` (xUnit v3 `TestContext` traits), so the full unit run is the check that no unit test reads the checkout, through any helper.
  - 34 local locator copies delegate to it; `AgentHelpTestPaths` reads only fixtures copied to the test output; the hard-coded `F:/GitHub/QBrainAi` fallback is removed. `UnitSuitePlatformScanner.FindRepositoryLocators` flags any other walk-up from the test output or a `CallerFilePath`, a relative walk-up, or a build-metadata read; its production binding `UnitProjects_HoldNoRepositoryLocatorBesideTheSharedOne` is an integration test. The two source-reading guards, `IngestionAllowlistContractTests.TestSources_DoNotDeclareSkippedXunitTests` and the `UnitSuitePlatformScannerTests` class, read the checkout and are integration tests on both hosts, as the chosen option required ("move the four named tests plus the two source-reading guards to Integration so no unit exception remains"); their scripted cases (`UnitSuitePlatformScannerMockGateTests`) stay unit tests.
  - BDPv4: `TestRepositoryRootPolicyTests` (9 scripted trait sets, negative controls, the ambient refusal, the integration binding) and 6 scripted locator cases with negative controls; mock gates `ug-r3-dry-mockgate-1` and `-2` green (Support.Mcp 3235 passed) before any locator was bound.
  - Binding: `ug-r3-dry-binding-1` was red by design, 172 results refused by the locator and 0 other failures. Those 163 test methods, 5 that create filesystem links, and 5 that read host state (current directory, home folder, filesystem root) are tagged `Category=Integration` with assertions unchanged. AgentPool, Voice and GitHubCli placeholders that used the current directory use the test output folder instead. Repl.Core.Tests and QBAgent.Tests join MigrationIntegrationTests (TR-MCP-NUKE-019 AC-2).
  - Moves by name: `runs/ug-r3-unit-moves.txt` lists the 191 results that left the unit run and the 20 new unit cases.
  - Limits, stated: the scanner reads one file's syntax; a helper in another file, a global using in another file, reflection, or OS behavior of a base-library call is outside it. Build.Tests, the Validation projects and the IntegrationTests projects keep their own walk-ups (outside the unit rule; open item).
- R2-F3: the Windows lane runner is the static `remote/Invoke-WinLaneRunner.ps1`, analyzed before upload; `Start-WinInteractiveLane.ps1` takes the commit, run id, task name and the runner's SHA-256 as parameters and refuses a runner whose hash differs. Every remote script runs over SSH as kingd through `Invoke-LegionSsh.ps1`, whose arguments are checked as plain literal tokens.

## 16. The user-profile scan fix (2026-10-20)

- Defect (triage `triage-report-b0e41c36f93c4c91b1bf2524eb579ef8`): the caveman plugin's `compress/scripts` is a relative symbolic link, and Windows refuses to traverse it as kingd ("The path cannot be traversed because it contains an untrusted mount point"). `MarkerFileService.EnumerateUserPluginCacheRoots` and `SessionLogTurnContextExtractor.AppendHistory` walked the profile with `SearchOption.AllDirectories`, so the `IOException` failed marker generation (19 tests), every import submit (7), and the callers that catch it (6 assertion failures).
- Operator choice (2026-10-20): "Fix scans in PR 87 (Recommended)": a BDPv4 bug fix in src so both scans skip links and IO errors, an AC on each owning TR, mock trees first, hermetic marker tests through the existing `AgentPluginUserProfileOverride` seam (replaced by `AgentPluginVersionSourcesOverride` in round 4, section 17), and the import-path session-log tests tagged Integration. The option text said 19 and 13; the stacks give 20 marker-path tests (19 plus `WorkspaceProcessManagerStartupTests`) and 12 import-path tests.
- Requirements first: AC-TR-MCP-WS-005-001 and AC-TR-MCP-SESSIONLOG-006-009 added through the plugin route (read back at 10:03).
- One walk, `DirectoryTreeWalker` (QBrainAi.Services), serves both scans: breadth first; a directory link or other reparse point is neither yielded nor descended; a directory whose listing throws `IOException` or `UnauthorizedAccessException` is yielded but not descended; file links are yielded as files; any other error propagates. LAB-OMARCHY has no mcpserver plugin root that is a link (`Find-ProfileLinks.ps1`), so resolution here is unchanged. (Extended in round 6, section 19, finding R5-F1: the walk checked links only among the entries it listed, so a scan root that is a link, or one reached through a link below the profile or home, was walked; `IsReachable` now checks the root and every folder between.)
- BDPv4: `DirectoryTreeWalkerTests` (7 scripted trees, a scripted-tree self check, negative controls for the legacy recursive walk, a link-following walk, a stop-at-first-error walk, a drop-unlistable walk and a catch-all walk) and `HermeticUserProfileFixtureTests` ran green before any production change: mock gate `ug-r3-scan-mockgate-1`, Nuke Test 4432 total, 4432 passed, 0 failed, 0 not executed.
- Production binding: the same 7 cases bound to `DirectoryTreeWalker`, error propagation, and `UserProfileScanBindingTests` for both scans, each with a seam control (the link flag removed changes the result). `FileSystemWalk_RealLinks_AreFlaggedAndNotDescended` is an integration test with real symbolic links; kingd can create them (Developer Mode on, probe `ug-r3-win-symlink-probe-1`). Binding run `ug-r3-scan-binding-1`: 4444 total, 4444 passed, 0 failed, 0 not executed.
- Hermetic marker tests: `HermeticUserProfileFixture` (`[assembly: AssemblyFixture]`) points plugin-version resolution at an empty temp profile for the whole Support.Mcp.Tests assembly; `WithHermeticPluginVersionResolution` restores the previous value instead of clearing it. Superseded in round 4 (section 17, finding R3-F1): the profile was only one of three inputs; the fixture now installs `AgentPluginVersionSources` with no plugin-root variable, no sibling folder and the empty profile, and `WithHermeticPluginVersionResolution` and `WithClearedPluginVersionEnvironment` are removed.
- Import-path tests: every import submit runs the history scan under the real home, so the 12 that failed as kingd are tagged Integration. The census is of failures; a unit test that reaches the scan with the real home and still passes as kingd would not appear in it (stated limit).

Beside it xUnit logged "Received unparseable output from test process: waiting for server to start....", followed by the JSON of a test-passed message. All 50 unparseable lines of that run are pg_ctl output ("waiting for server to start", "shut down", "done", "server started", "server stopped").
  - Cause: xUnit v3 runs the test project as its own process and sends its messages as JSON lines on that process's standard output. `EphemeralPostgresFixture` runs pg_ctl without output redirection (a pipe hangs the caller, because the postgres child inherits it), so pg_ctl's informational lines land in that stream and garble messages; a garbled test-passed loses the result. Only the Windows lane starts pg_ctl: Linux uses a provisioned PostgreSQL (TR-MCP-NUKE-018), and the unit run has no PostgreSQL case.
  - Probe `ug-r3-win-pgctl-silent-probe-2` (PostgreSQL 17 on PAYTON-LEGION2): pg_ctl start and stop print 54 and 58 characters to standard output without `-s` and 0 with it, exit 0 both ways.

### 19.2 R5-F1: links at and above the scan root (BDPv4, production fix)

- Tests first, in `DirectoryTreeWalkerTests` (unit; scripted listing fake, host-neutral): a reachability contract for a root below a boundary (the user profile or home), checked through the walker's existing listing seam (each folder from the boundary down to the root must be listed by its parent as a directory that is not a link). Cases: a normal root (reachable); the root a link; each ancestor below the boundary a link (`.codex`, `.codex/plugins`, `.codex/plugins/cache`, `.claude`, `.claude/plugins`, `.grok`); the boundary itself a link (still reachable); a parent listing that throws `IOException` or `UnauthorizedAccessException` (not reachable); a root missing from its parent's listing (not reachable); any other listing error propagates. Negative controls inside the passing harness: the current unchecked root, a check of the root only, a check that also rejects a linked boundary, and a check that follows links. Mock gate run `ug-r6-reach-mockgate-1` (full Nuke Test, test-run id) green before any production change.
- Production: `DirectoryTreeWalker` gains the reachability check over the same listing function; `MarkerFileService.EnumerateUserPluginCacheRoots` checks each of the three cache roots against the user profile, and `SessionLogTurnContextExtractor.AppendHistory` checks each of the four history roots against home, before walking. An unreachable root contributes nothing; fallback and unexpected-error behavior are unchanged.
- Binding: the same cases and controls bound to the production check; `UserProfileScanBindingTests` (unit, temporary profile, scripted link flags) gains resolve-level cases: each cache root linked and each of the `.codex`, `.claude` and `.grok` ancestors linked falls back to the next candidate or the synced version, a linked `.claude` history root keeps None, and a linked profile is still scanned; each with the seam control (the link flag removed changes the result). Integration (both lanes, no Platform tag): `FileSystemWalk_RealRootAndAncestorLinks_AreNotTraversed` with real directory symbolic links in a temporary tree (kingd can create them on PAYTON-LEGION2, Developer Mode on).
- Unit and integration facts: the new contract and binding cases are unit tests on scripted data or temporary folders; the real-link case is an integration test in both lanes.

### 19.5 What-if pass (for this section)

- Ordering and concurrency: env-writing tests in a parallel run were a latent race (two classes setting `QBRAINAI_INSTANCE` and `MCP_INSTANCE`); closed by the collection.
- Boundary: a profile or home that is itself a link (for example a home under `/var/home`) is still scanned; a root that vanishes between `Directory.Exists` and the check is unreachable, not an error.
- Each host: directory symbolic links and junctions both carry the reparse-point attribute, which the listing seam reads on Windows and Linux alike.
- Isolation: explicit plugin roots (the plugin-root variables and the workspace sibling folder) are named inputs, not profile scans, and stay unchecked by design; the agent transcript file passed by the caller is a named file, not part of the history scan.
- Operator-visible output: none changes; an unreachable root contributes nothing, as a link inside it already did.
- Each test project (added while building): a writer outside the seven unit projects (`QBrainAi.PluginIntegration.Tests`) is already serialized; the other test projects run in their own processes, so a write there cannot reach a unit run.

### 20.8 What-if pass (for this section)

- A variable read only by the runtime (`XDG_DATA_HOME`): kept derived from the scripted HOME; a unit test that needs the real local data folder would fail the folder-mode run, which points HOME at the scripted home.
- A test that reaches a temp resolver on Windows: the helper's folder is under the official temporary folder, so the permission rule the strategies add lands on a test-owned folder that is deleted afterwards.
- A body that throws inside the helper: every name is restored and both folders deleted (bound by a test).
- A strategy that read its fallback instead of its primary: the fallback points at a different folder, so the primary-wins assertions (the configured temp folder test, the Codex configured-root case, the prompt and output file cases) fail; each class names its own primary.
- A caller that names a variable outside the chain: refused before any variable is written or any folder made (bound by a test).
- A unit test that reaches environment-reading production code but whose outcome cannot change (for example the REPL stream timeout, which the gate sets to 1 second): it stays a unit test under the amended criteria, and both gate runs are its proof. (Superseded by section 21: round 7 showed that one safe value does not prove the outcome cannot change, R7-F1; the criteria now require the numeric runs at 0 and at both 32-bit ends and the time zone and culture run, and the REPL timeouts are bound at their call sites.)
- A future test that reaches a resolver without the helper: the folder-mode and file-mode gate runs fail it, which is the proof the amended criteria require; the collection list is not discovered from source (stated limit, unchanged).

### 21.1 Requirements first

- TR-MCP-REPL-001 AC-002 (under FR-MCP-REPL-001), added through the plugin at 12:57:34 and read back (2 criteria, changed only AC-002, unsatisfied), during the self-evaluation and before any test: a command timeout configured through `MCPSERVER_REPL_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (agent stdio requests) or `MCPSERVER_REPL_STREAM_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (stream commands) is used only when it is a whole number of seconds from 1 to the largest delay the runtime's cancellation timer accepts (4294967 seconds); any other value gives the two-minute default, so no request fails and the process does not crash because of the configured value, and the timeout message names the timeout that applied; one parse rule serves both variables. The bound was measured on the runtime (`Test-CancelAfterLimit.ps1`: 4294967 s accepted, 4294968 s rejected) and is bound again by a unit test on the test runtime.

### 21.9 What-if pass (for this section)

- A configured timeout of exactly 4294967 s: used; `CancelAfter` accepts it (bound by the runtime check). 4294968 s and larger: the default, never an exception.
- A value with blanks or a leading plus sign: parsed as before (`NumberStyles.Integer`), since the earlier parser accepted the same forms; a decimal or a thousands separator: the default.
- A culture whose digits or signs differ (tr-TR): the parse uses the invariant culture; the time-zone and culture gate run is the proof.
- The variable changing while a command runs: the timeout is read once per command, so the message names the timeout that applied.
- The stream timeout: the same rule. Until 21.6 no unit test reached the stream path's `CancelAfter` with an out-of-range value; `ProductionStreamCommand_SatisfiesTheContract` now does (2147483647, 4294968, 4294967), and the max runs cover both names under the real environment.
- The variable read more than once per request (the message could then name a different value than the timer used): the call-site contract fails any call site that reads its variable other than exactly once per run.
- A call site reading the other call site's variable: the contract fails it in every row.
- A command that finishes in the same instant the timer fires: either outcome is valid; the contract uses only commands that answer at once or never, so no row depends on that race.
- A new call site that reads a REPL timeout variable: the search finds exactly two readers, both through `ReplCommandTimeout.Read`; a third would need its own binding to the contract.
- A future numeric variable read by production: the max, zero and min runs set every numeric variable in the gate's list; a new name joins the list through the one-time search, which stays a search, not a scanner.
- A junction alias that already exists: the fixture creates a fresh alias path per fixture; `CreateDirectory` on an existing empty folder would turn that folder into the junction, and a non-empty one fails the reparse call (`Win32Exception`), never silently.
- A target path with a space or longer than the sample: the field-by-field decode row covers a space; the lengths are checked against ushort (`checked`), so an oversized path throws instead of writing a wrong buffer.
- The fixture on Linux: unchanged, `Directory.CreateSymbolicLink`; the Windows binding is Platform=Windows and runs only in the Windows lane, and it throws, never returns, if it ever ran elsewhere.
- Other fixtures that start a process to make a link: the first search (the phrase `mklink /J`) missed `BugTriage139EighteenthTraversalTests`, which adds the command tokens one by one; the token search in 21.5 found it, it now uses `WindowsJunction.Create`, and the same search finds no other; every other real-link case uses `Directory.CreateSymbolicLink` or `File.CreateSymbolicLink` in process.
- Two local suites at once on the shared tmpfs `/tmp`: not done again after `ug-r8-linux-test-2`; local gates run one at a time.

(Removed in section 24: setting up the environment gets no criterion; the download fix stays as a fix under TR-MCP-NUKE-019.)

Mock gate `ug-r8b-download-mockgate-1` 5/6 (the contract did not turn a thrown download into an assertion; test fixed), `ug-r8b-download-mockgate-2` 6/6 with BuffersWholeBody, DefaultClientTimeout, DeadlineNotReset, NoInactivityLimit and UnnamedFailure rejected, before any production change [27.42, eighteenth read C10, slip: both mock gates (20:06:29 to 20:06:54 and 20:07:26 to 20:07:51) ran on this host while the hostile run `ug-r8b-gate-v6-locale-1` (20:04:11 to 20:08:12, exit 0) was still running in the probe worktree, against 21.5's rule that every local gate runs alone; the binding `ug-r8b-download-binding-1` (20:08:24) started after it ended; times from the run records in `runs-r8b/`].

- **The rule.** Operator, 2026-10-27, verbatim, in order:
  - "NO!  Setting up environment is not something that gets AC.  Stop this madness." This was the answer to my pre-HV proposal to strengthen TR-MCP-NUKE-019 AC-8; R8b-P3 is withdrawn.
  - "I have been very clear for several days:  Acceptance Criteria are not bound to development environment."
  - After stopping my first removal attempt, when only dry runs had run: "Let's be clear again: UNIT TESTS may not be bound to an environment.  Integration tests may be, but only run on that environment."
  - "More clarification: Acceptance Criteria are for defining UNIT TESTS that must be covered as TDD during development.  That inherently excludes ENVIRONMENT specific criteria."
  - "Finish updates" and "Then continue PR87 remediation."
- **Requirements, through the plugin.** Each change ran as a dry run first and was read back; every other criterion of each record is unchanged.

## 25. Develop drift, the cline-qb move, and the docs-only merge (2026-10-28)

All times are local (America/Chicago, UTC-5); the operator's messages and answers are read from the session transcript (`Find-TranscriptText.ps1`, read only).

### 25.1 Develop drift at the round 8b launch

- The operator confirmed the round 8b list R8b-P1 to R8b-P17 ("Confirm, launch HV 8b (Recommended)", answered 15:32:53). My pre-launch check then found that origin develop had moved since this branch's base `3da2db93`: five first-parent commits from 2026-10-22 to 2026-10-27, and a test merge with 11 conflicted files. I had not looked at that content before asking. Asked at 15:35:28; operator: "Merge develop first, then HV (Recommended)" (answered 17:47:49), whose option text was: merge develop into PR 87 and resolve the 11 conflicts keeping both sides' behavior; a real behavior clash comes back to the operator.
- The merge was started at `4634c73c` against develop `d3df5353`. Three files were resolved by hand, and six of the remaining eight were whole-file conflicts: develop had changed their line endings (CRLF to LF in five test files, LF to CRLF in `.nuke/build.schema.json`) on top of small edits (`Get-EolStats.ps1`).
- Operator, 17:58:21 (verbatim): "Git is supposed to ignore line endings anywhere in the lab environment."
  - Found: both lab hosts set `core.autocrlf=false`, `core.safecrlf=false`, `core.whitespace=cr-at-eol` and `merge.renormalize=true`, but no file had a `text` attribute, so renormalization did nothing. A throwaway repository reproduced the conflict (`Test-GitEolMerge.ps1`, per-command `git -c` overrides only).
  - For the open merge, each conflicted file's three stages were merged again with their line endings normalized to the ones ours used (`Merge-EolNormalized.ps1`, dry run first). Five merged cleanly, each differing from HEAD only by develop's real edits. Three still had real content conflicts.
  - Asked 18:12:02 with two questions; answered 18:15:38: "Global text=auto (Recommended)" and, for the clash below, "Strengthen AC, use sources (Recommended)". My option text for the first question omitted that `* text=auto` alone makes a Windows checkout write CRLF for every LF-stored text file (`core.eol` defaults to native). A second throwaway test (`Test-GitEolCheckout.ps1`) showed it, and that `* text=auto eol=lf` keeps checkouts LF on both hosts while merging clean. Asked again at 18:49:02; operator: "* text=auto eol=lf (Recommended)" (answered 18:49:36). Applied at 18:55 to the global attributes file on both hosts (`Set-GitGlobalAttributes.ps1`, dry run first; `git check-attr` reports `text: auto` and `eol: lf`; the checked checkouts' status line counts unchanged). This is lab configuration, not a change to this repository.
- The behavior clash: develop's `MarkerFileService.BuildConfiguredClineQbContract` read `CLINE_QB_PLUGIN_ROOT` and `CLINE_QB_PLUGIN_ARTIFACT_SHA256` straight from the process environment, bypassing the injected `AgentPluginVersionSources`, against AC-TR-MCP-WS-005-001. Its three new tests wrote process variables through a helper this branch had removed. The chosen strengthening of AC-TR-MCP-WS-005-001 was never written and no code was changed. The operator asked "Why are you concerned with ClineQB?" (18:16:58) and "Describe this, I don't remember authorizing it." (18:28:42), and develop was then reset (25.2). The clash no longer reaches this branch, and AC-TR-MCP-WS-005-001 is unchanged since 2026-10-27T22:25:32 (until 27.1, which moved it to FR-MCP-018 ac-1; sixteenth holistic read finding c6).

### 25.2 The cline-qb move and the restored develop

It entered develop through `02933e74` (2026-10-21, "P1 acceptance and full phase validation remain pending. No new test suite was run for this checkpoint operation.") and the merge `4d034824` (2026-10-27 16:12, "Operator-authorized stop and sync. ### 26.2 Slice D: explicit-baseline environment deltas (R8B-F1; production seam, BDPv4)

- Tests first, in `QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Tests` (unit; maps only, no process environment): an environment-delta contract over a requested map and a baseline lookup. Rows: a changed token is kept (`ExtractEnvironment_ChangedTokenIsIncluded`); an unchanged token is omitted (`ExtractEnvironment_UnchangedTokenIsOmitted`); unchanged defaults are omitted while a changed path is kept (`ExtractEnvironment_DefaultVariablesAreOmitted`); a variable absent from the baseline is kept; a null requested value is skipped; a value that differs only in case is kept (ordinal); no deltas gives null. The scripted reference passes; wrong builders are rejected inside a passing harness: one that keeps every requested value, one that omits by key presence alone, one that compares values ignoring case, and one that returns an empty map instead of null. Mock gate: before any production change, shared with slice E as one full Nuke Test (`ug-r9-mockgate-de-1`), since no filtered Nuke route then existed for one unit class and raw `dotnet test` was kept for Build.Tests (since slice Q, 27.20, the Nuke target TestGroups runs selected classes; sixteenth holistic read finding a10). As built: `tests/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Tests/Services/EnvironmentDeltaContractTests.cs`.
- Production: `DesktopProcessSpawner.ExtractEnvironment(requested, baseline)` (internal) holds the comparison; the existing `ExtractEnvironment(ProcessStartInfo)` delegates with the process environment as the baseline, so production behavior is unchanged. No public signature changes.
- Binding: the same rows on the production overload. The integration case keeps its assertions, owns its GH_TOKEN baseline (sets it to a value other than the child's and restores it), joins `ProcessEnvironmentCollection`, and joins the writer list in `ProcessEnvironmentCollectionTests`. Its PATH literal no longer names a lab user (`C:\Users\example\...`).

### 26.3 Slice E: the owned-instance environment (R8B-F3 and the consumer context; test support, BDPv4)

- Tests first, in `QBrainAi.Repl.Core.Tests/TestSupport/OwnedInstanceEnvironmentTests.cs` (unit; maps and strings only; the consumer fact is in `TemporaryInstanceChecksTests.cs`): a contract over two new pure functions in `tests/TestSupport.Shared/TemporaryInstanceChecks.cs`:
  - building the child environment from the inherited map and the instance's own settings: every inherited key in the product's configuration and variable namespaces (`Mcp__`, `QBrainAi__`, `MCP_`, `QBRAINAI_`, `MCPSERVER_`, `ConnectionStrings__`, compared ignoring case) and the provider fallbacks `DATABASE_URL` and `POSTGRES_CONNECTION_STRING` are removed, the instance's own settings are applied, and everything else is kept;
  - checking the child environment before start: every product-namespace value is one the instance set, and every database selection it set lies inside the owned root; otherwise the check names the violation.
  - Rows, named after the reviewer's tests: an inherited external connection string is removed (`OwnedInstanceConfiguration_RejectsExternalConnectionString`); canonical and legacy overrides, separately and together, cannot change the owned database (`OwnedInstanceConfiguration_CanonicalAndLegacyOverridesCannotEscape`); an inherited provider and an inherited instance name are removed; the owned database stays selected exactly (`OwnedInstanceConfiguration_OwnedDatabaseRemainsSelected`); unrelated variables (PATH, HOME, DOTNET_ settings) are kept; and the start check accepts the owned layout and refuses a database outside the owned root, a sibling root sharing its prefix, and an inherited product key left in place. The consumer context (`TemporaryInstanceConsumer_UsesExplicitOwnedWorkspaceAndMarker`) reuses the existing start-argument rows. Wrong builders and checks are rejected inside a passing harness: a builder that keeps inherited values, one that removes only the legacy form, one that drops the instance's own settings (refuses everything), one that matches prefixes case-sensitively, one that keeps the provider fallbacks, a check that accepts anything, one that checks only the database paths, and one that compares a string prefix without a segment boundary. Mock gate: Nuke Test (`ug-r9-mockgate-de-1`, with slice D), green, 0 not executed, before the test support changes. The builder compares keys ignoring case, as the mock-gated reference does; stated limit: on Linux two inherited non-product variables whose names differ only in case collapse to one. Superseded in 27.31 (slice Z4 part 3, seventh holistic read findings g1 and b3): ac-4 as strengthened on 2026-11-05 says unrelated variables are kept unchanged, so the builder now compares names ordinally and keeps both names of such a pair; the limit no longer holds.
- Test support: one starter, `tests/TestSupport.Shared/OwnedInstanceProcess.cs` (`ApplyOwnedEnvironment`: builds the child environment with the builder, refuses it with the check before the process starts, then replaces the start environment), kept out of `TemporaryInstanceChecks`, whose functions stay pure. `ScratchQBrainAi` starts its server through it and sets its database in both the canonical and legacy forms (provider and connection string) besides the YAML; the REPL children of `ReplChildProcessHelper` and of `RequirementScopeLayerReplIntegrationTests` start through it, and `RequirementScopeLayerReplIntegrationTests` checks its REPL start arguments with `CheckReplStartArguments` before the process starts.
- Binding: `SharedOwnedEnvironment_SatisfiesTheContract` and the reviewer's three named facts on the shared functions; the lanes on both hosts are the runtime binding.

### 26.4 Slice F: the two Support integration classes in the lane (R8B-F2; build code, BDPv4)

- Tests first, in Build.Tests: `IntegrationLaneProjectsContract` gains QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.IntegrationTests as the seventh lane project, after QBrainAi.Repl.IntegrationTests, with its class selection (`ClassesOf`, a default interface member so the build compiles during the mock gate: the two class names, in AC-2's order, and none for the six others, which run whole); a solution that lacks it is refused. A filter-composition contract (`IIntegrationLaneClassFilter`, `IntegrationLaneClassFilterContract`) gives, for a host filter and a class list, the host filter alone for an empty list and otherwise `<host filter>&(FullyQualifiedName~.<class>.|...)`: parenthesized because `&` binds tighter than `|`, and each term bounded by dots so a class name cannot match a longer one; a blank host filter and a class name that is not a plain identifier are refused. Wrong selectors and composers are rejected inside a passing harness: the five or six projects only (the selections before slice A and before this slice), every class of the Support integration project, a third class, the two classes named for every project, the Acid project, and the earlier faults; a composer without the parentheses, without the dot bounds, joining with `&`, dropping the host filter, ignoring the classes, accepting any name, or refusing an empty list. Mock gate: Build.Tests filtered to the two mock-gate classes (`ug-r9-mockgate-f-1`), green.
- Production: `IntegrationLaneProjects` returns the seven projects and the class selection; `IntegrationLaneClassFilter` composes; `MigrationIntegrationTests` composes each project's filter and logs every project's filter besides the host filter (AC-3).
- Binding: the same rows on the production code, the solution-file row check, a check that each of the two classes is declared once in the project's sources and tagged Category=Integration (`SupportIntegrationClasses_AreDeclaredOnceAndTaggedIntegration`), and the lane filters composed from the production pieces on both hosts (`LaneFilters_ComposeFromTheProductionPieces`); then the Linux lane, which runs the two classes for the first time at this branch's head. A failure of either class comes back to the operator before round 9, as the chosen option says.

- Tests first, in `QBrainAi.Repl.Core.Tests/TestSupport/OwnedDatabaseSelectionTests.cs` (unit; strings only): a contract over one new pure check, `TemporaryInstanceChecks.CheckDatabaseSelection`: the connection string the instance's database context holds names a Data Source (keyword ignoring case; the last one wins, as in a connection string builder) strictly inside the owned root, segment by segment with no '.' or '..' segment; a missing connection string, a server connection string with no Data Source, the owned root itself, a sibling root sharing its prefix, a parent-segment escape and a repeated Data Source whose last value is outside are refused. Wrong checks rejected inside a passing harness: one that accepts anything, one where the first Data Source wins, a string-prefix comparison, a case-sensitive keyword, and one that accepts a missing connection string. A check after `Build` in `CreateHost` was rejected: with minimal hosting the application starts its own host on its entry-point thread, so it would race the start; under the Test environment `Program.cs` opens no database between `Build` and `Run` (`Program.cs:805`), so the guard runs before anything opens the database [27.41, seventeenth holistic read finding a10: false; see the correction of 26.6 at the start of section 27, fixed in 27.2].

The proof sits in a lane class because a new Support integration class would not run under AC-2's class selection.

### 26.8 What-if pass (for this section)

- A parent environment that holds `QBRAINAI_INSTANCE` or `MCP_INSTANCE`: removed by the namespace rule, so no instance-specific database setting can apply.
- A parent `DATABASE_URL` with the provider left at its default: the provider is set explicitly to SQLite in both forms, and the variable is removed.
- A Windows parent whose variable names differ only in case (`Qbrainai_...`): the namespace match ignores case.
- The baseline lookup on Windows, where variable names are case-insensitive: the production overload keeps the existing lookup (`Environment.GetEnvironmentVariables()[key]`), so its behavior does not change on either host.
- A future lane class added without a contract row: the selection contract pins the exact class list, so the binding fails.
- `QuadBrainOllamaEndpointIntegrationTests` failing its live-model rows again: stopped and taken to the operator before round 9; it is not narrowed or skipped.

### 27.8 Slice O: the lane-2 migration-lock failure (LANE-1)

- Diagnosis (`diag-O-sqlserver-migration-lock-20261029.md`): the failing error is SqlException 1223 from EF Core's lock release in `Migrator.MigrateImplementation`'s finally block; EF takes the lock with `@LockOwner = 'Session'`. The original diagnosis inferred a connection-closing failure and a replaced session; that causal inference was not demonstrated and is withdrawn. Subsequent first-failure instrumentation reproduced a release-only failure without an earlier EF command/connection event. The root cause remains unknown; 27.15 and 27.16 hold the execution contract and receipts.
- Fix, in BDPv4 order, against FR-MCP-TESTRESOURCE-001 ac-5: the SQL Server migration tests report the first command or connection failure EF met, with the release error attached (unit contract with a scripted failure sequence; wrong reporters that keep only the last error or drop the release error rejected; mock gate; then the test support; then the binding). Then the diagnostic repeats the operator chose ("Diagnostic repeats (Recommended)", 2026-10-30): the four SQL Server migration classes about 50 times with raw `dotnet test` against the Linux test container, a one-off exception to tests-only-through-Nuke for diagnosis, never a gate, each run's TRX kept. Then the revealed cause is fixed through BDPv4. If it does not recur, the HV request says so plainly and the flake is not claimed fixed.
- The first-failure reporting needs its own criterion (a test-harness rule, so on FR-MCP-TESTRESOURCE-001 under the 2026-10-30 ruling); its text goes to the operator for approval before slice O's tests are written, because BDPv4 starts from the criterion. The product's own startup-migration behavior is PLAN-STARTUPMIGRATION-001, not this slice.

### 27.11 What-if pass (for this section)

- A test override that replaces the database context after the factory's check is registered: the check is registered last, after the test's own overrides, so it sees the final connection string.
- A REPL child with a marker that resolves: it still gets `MCP_SERVER_URL` from the starter, and the start check refuses a foreign address.
- A lane project whose class list matches a renamed class: the filter selects no case and the lane fails (BLD-1), not passes.
- The migration-lock failure recurring under the first-failure reporting: the reported first failure names the cause; it is fixed in slice O before round 9, or taken to the operator if it lies outside the branch.
- A gh call for a native workspace with no repository configured: unchanged, it runs in the workspace.
- A placement move that leaves a test citing the old id: ValidateTraceability and the citation search after the moves catch it.

### 27.15 Slice O execution contract (acceptance still pending)

- Live layer-1 FR-MCP-TESTRESOURCE-001 ac-5 was read back before tests. Scope is test support for SQL Server only; no product startup-migration change, public API, schema, backfill or deployment change. Breaking-change flag false.
- One per-test reporter captures EF CommandError and ConnectionError events through the existing structured LogTo callback, without global listeners or process state. Each synchronous/asynchronous migration operation starts a fresh collection. On a failed operation it reports the first observed exception first, then every distinct later exception including the escaping lock-release error. A failure with no diagnostic event still reports its escaping exception. Successful operations preserve success; failures from earlier operations cannot leak into later reports. Cancellation remains cancellation with diagnostics attached. The reporter never swallows an operation failure or retries a migration.
- Tests first: independent scripted reporter, first-command/first-connection plus release sequences, multiple later errors, no-event failure, duplicate event identity, successful operation and reset between operations, synchronous/asynchronous parity and cancellation. Wrong reporters that retain only the last error, discard the later release error, or leak a previous operation must be rejected by the same assertions inside a passing harness. Pin the full Nuke mock gate before implementing the reporter. Bind the same assertions to the real helper, plus prove structured EF event capture on an owned in-memory connection without contacting a SQL server. Wire the helper into the four serialized SQL Server migration classes; direct captured-SQL header batches use the same operation wrapper, while the three EF-based classes attach the structured event callback to their context options.
- Then run the approved 50 raw dotnet diagnostic repeats against the provisioned Linux SQL Server container. Each repeat retains its complete TRX and command. They are diagnosis, never a replacement gate. A nonrecurrence leaves the original cause unproven and must be disclosed before HV. Any demonstrated root-cause repair receives its own tests-first increment.

A test-armed cancellation or timeout is a wall-clock wait when its firing changes what the code under test does (for example it cancels or times out the operation whose outcome the test asserts) and an asserted step must happen before it fires. "Far above" means at least 100 times the longest real or scripted delay of the awaited step; a step with no real or scripted delay meets it for any positive timeout ...".
  - FR-MCP-TESTRESOURCE-001 generated-artifact criterion (position 1 [27.42, eighteenth read C18: the receipts' index, counted from 0; it is the record's second criterion of 12, with a blank id, per the plugin getter on 2026-11-06 at 14:47:01]), appended at 16:37:48: "The UNC server and share names are held to the same segment rule: a UNC folder whose server or share name ends in a dot or a space or holds a colon is refused, because Windows may resolve it to the checkout's server or share ...".

- **Declared at `52e6d627`** (adds to the lists of 27.24 to 27.28; 27.30 adds to all of them, a sibling of sixteenth holistic read finding a6):
  - The startup health wait as built (operator: "Keep, out of D-B"): it counts 120 probes 5 s apart rather than an elapsed 10 minutes. Each probe is one HTTP request of up to 3 s plus one `systemctl show`, which `RunCommand` bounds only at 15 minutes (`LinuxServiceHelper.cs:57`), so the wait has no fixed upper bound near 10 minutes: 120 slow requests and 119 sleeps alone make about 16 minutes, and slow state reads add to that. Any ActiveState other than active, reloading and activating after an automatic restart included, ends it as a stop. `ProbeHealth` treats every `HttpRequestException` (refused, reset, name resolution, protocol or TLS) and every `TaskCanceledException` as "not yet", and a non-success status as unhealthy, so only another exception type from the probe propagates; a failing or timed-out `systemctl show` propagates too, and the failure path then stops the unit. No test binds `StartupHealthProbes`, `StartupHealthInterval`, `ProbeHealth` or the call in `UpdateLinuxService`; the two constants carry no dated change log of their own (finding c7). Kept wording: the `UpdateLinuxService` doc comment ("up to 10 minutes"), the `StartupHealthProbes` summary ("10 minutes at the interval"), the `WaitForStartupHealth` remark ("93 s at 14:27", a journal reading with no receipt), the contract row name "95 s at 5 s, the measured startup", and in the stored criterion "93 s on 2026-11-04 at 14:27" and "an 18 s health window". This item said until the sixth holistic read that a probe exception other than a refused or timed-out request propagates and that a stalling server holds the wait near 16 minutes; both were wrong (sixth holistic finding g3). startup-health-wait is not set satisfied after AGREE.
  - The Windows update keeps its 10 probes 2 s apart health window (finding d4; triage above).
  - A failure part-way through the replacement can leave neither apphost in the install; the recovery then keeps the restored unit (no binary rollback), as failed-update-startable states.
  - `timing-sweep-classification-r12z3.psd1` header line 17 still says "the five at exactly 100 times"; six bounds (seven cases) are (finding f4); the classification is the sweep's input and is not rewritten.
  - The task 45 notice for the health-wait commit (text stamped 17:42, written 17:43) names "about 95 s" for the startup (finding c4); the stored TODO text is kept, and 27.28 states the receipted times.
  - The deploy and start `.log` files stay untracked beside their committed `.out.txt` copies.
  - The recovery's retarget as built (sixth holistic finding g1; operator: "Declare, keep in D-B"): `RetargetLegacyExecStart` decides from the effective ExecStart read before the update and then replaces the name `McpServer.Support.Mcp` everywhere in the unit file and in every drop-in, as the success path has done since before PR 87. So a non-ExecStart line that holds the name (Description, Environment, EnvironmentFile, ReadWritePaths) is rewritten too, and an install folder whose path holds the name would have WorkingDirectory and the ExecStart folder rewritten to a folder that does not exist; two legacy drop-ins are each rewritten, but no row has two; a legacy fragment overridden by a drop-in that names the current apphost is left as restored, which the stored text's "a unit file or drop-in that names the legacy apphost ... is retargeted" does not separate from "a unit that already names QBrainAi.Support.Mcp is restored unchanged"; and the recovery does not run `systemctl reset-failed`, so a unit left at systemd's start limit stays there. The six contract rows hold only ExecStart lines and at most one drop-in. failed-update-startable is still set satisfied after AGREE on those six rows.
  - The recovery's report as built (sixth holistic finding g2; operator: "Declare"): when the restore completes and the retarget or the daemon-reload after it fails, the update throws "Update and live-state restoration failed" with both exceptions, although the restore completed; at the runtime-configuration failure point systemd may then still hold the legacy unit until someone reloads by hand, and the message does not say so. The binding asserts only that the update throws (`Assert.ThrowsAny<Exception>`), and the command double's daemon-reload never fails, so no row covers a failing recovery step.

- **Criteria first**, through the plugin (`Set-RequirementCriterion.ps1`, each dry run first, each readback showing only that criterion changed; the store receipt `ug-r13z4b-store-readback-1.json`, read 05:01:30, compared with `ug-r12z3c-store-readback-1.json` in `ug-r13z4b-store-compare-z3c-z4b.txt`: across TR-MCP-SERVICEUPDATE-001, TR-MCP-USECASE-018 and FR-MCP-TESTRESOURCE-001 only ac-4 and AC-006 changed, each as an append to its 2026-11-04 text, IMPL-UNITGREEN-001 gained tasks 47 and 48 and nothing else, and IMPL-SERVICEOVERRIDES-001 is new):
  - FR-MCP-TESTRESOURCE-001 ac-4 (unit-proved, so on the FR), appended at 02:50:54 and narrowed at 02:53:18 and 02:55:35, now ending: "A setting is identified by its configuration key, not by its spelling: .NET configuration reads a process-environment name with a double underscore standing for the ':' delimiter and case ignored, and a DOTNET_ or ASPNETCORE_ prefix places the rest of the name in the host's configuration, so every colon, double-underscore or mixed-separator spelling of a key, alone or host-prefixed, sets that key. No inherited spelling of a product key or of the host's endpoint section (Kestrel), in either enumeration order of the inherited environment, changes the instance's effective database, workspace, marker or server address; unrelated variables are kept unchanged; and a start environment is refused before the instance starts when it holds a product key the instance did not set, under any spelling, or two names of one product key that differ only in separators or case (hostile validation round 12 finding R12-F1; ...)." The first wording would have refused the instance's own `DOTNET_ENVIRONMENT` and `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT` pair, and the second would have refused unrelated inherited duplicates; both narrowings were made before any code.

- **g1 (material)**: `TemporaryInstanceChecks.BuildOwnedInstanceEnvironment` built the child in an ignore-case map, so on Linux two unrelated inherited names that differ only in case merged into one, against ac-4's "unrelated variables are kept unchanged"; the case clause of the two-name refusal could not be reached, and the contract's helpers could not hold such a pair. My sibling sweep of the consumers (`Find-CodeLines.ps1` over tests) found two more of the class: `OwnedReplStart.CreateStartInfo` checked an ignore-case copy of the start environment, which would throw on such a pair once the builder kept it; and the instance's own settings outside the product's namespaces (`PORT`, `DOTNET_NOLOGO`, `DOTNET_CLI_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT`, the stdio host's logging levels) had no spelling rule, so an inherited `port` kept the owned value under the inherited name and an inherited `Logging:LogLevel:Default` survived beside the owned `Logging__LogLevel__Default`.
- **Criterion first**: FR-MCP-TESTRESOURCE-001 ac-4 appended through the plugin (`Set-RequirementCriterion.ps1`, data `crit-z4c-testresource-ac4.psd1`, dry run 06:17:20, write 06:17:32, readback: 12 criteria, only ac-4 changed): "A name is matched exactly, as the process environment holds it: two inherited names that differ only in case are two variables, and each unrelated one is kept with its own value. An inherited name that sets the same configuration key as one of the instance's own settings, under any spelling, is a competing value for that setting, not an unrelated variable: the instance's setting replaces it, and a start environment that holds two names of a key the instance sets that differ only in separators or case is refused, as two names of one product key are (seventh holistic read before hostile validation round 13, finding g1; ...)." It reaches past the earlier outcomes (database, workspace, marker and address) to every key the instance sets, so it is an append, under the operator's "Strengthening Acceptance Criteria should always be the recommended option" and "don't gate on me between rounds", as at 02:50 today.

- **g1 (material)**: `SetsOwnedKey` compared an inherited name with the instance's own settings by `ConfigurationKeyIdentity` alone (separators and case), so an inherited `NOLOGO` or `CLI_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT` was kept beside the instance's `DOTNET_NOLOGO` and `DOTNET_CLI_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT` (`ScratchQBrainAi.cs:265-266`, `OwnedReplStart.cs:34-35`), and the later environment layer gave the child the inherited value. Stored ac-4 already says that "a DOTNET_ or ASPNETCORE_ prefix places the rest of the name in the host's configuration, so every colon, double-underscore or mixed-separator spelling of a key, alone or host-prefixed, sets that key", and its 06:17:32 append makes such a name a competing value the instance's setting replaces. The code was narrower than the stored criterion, so no criterion changed and the fix is in code. Practical risk was low (no database, workspace, marker or address key is reachable this way), but section 6, the PR body, the handoff and the brief's UG48 said every inherited spelling was removed (finding e3).

So the run-time check counts every agent execution session the service creates through a test-side decorator of the resolver, rather than reflection over the private map or a production accessor made only for a test.
